# pytest cache directory #

This directory contains data from the pytest's cache plugin,
which provides the `--lf` and `--ff` options, as well as the `cache` fixture.

**Do not** commit this to version control.

See [the docs](https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/how-to/cache.html) for more information.


================================================================================
FILE: compose.yaml
================================================================================

services:
  # ── SearXNG (self-hosted search) ───────────────────────────────────────
  searxng:
    image: searxng/searxng:latest
    container_name: local_agent_searxng
    ports:
      - "127.0.0.1:8080:8080"
    volumes:
      - ./searxng:/etc/searxng:rw
    restart: unless-stopped
    security_opt:
      - no-new-privileges:true
    cap_drop:
      - ALL

  # ── Agent Sandbox (Linux workstation) ──────────────────────────────────
  sandbox:
    build:
      context: ./sandbox
      dockerfile: Dockerfile
    container_name: xalgv_sandbox
    ports:
      - "127.0.0.1:9000:9000"   # Tool server API
      - "127.0.0.1:6080:6080"   # noVNC (watch the agent live)
    volumes:
      - ./workspace:/workspace:rw
    restart: unless-stopped
    security_opt:
      - no-new-privileges:true
    cap_drop:
      - ALL
    mem_limit: 4g
    cpus: 2.0
    init: true
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "python3", "/opt/tool_server/healthcheck.py"]
      interval: 15s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 5
      start_period: 15s
    environment:
      - DISPLAY=:99
      - XVFB_RESOLUTION=1280x720x24
      - XBROWSER_SCOPE=${XBROWSER_SCOPE:-default}
      - XBROWSER_ALLOWED_DOMAINS=${XBROWSER_ALLOWED_DOMAINS:-}
      - XBROWSER_DENIED_DOMAINS=${XBROWSER_DENIED_DOMAINS:-}
      - XBROWSER_LOCAL_ORIGINS=${XBROWSER_LOCAL_ORIGINS:-}
      - XBROWSER_ADMIN_TOKEN=${XBROWSER_ADMIN_TOKEN:-}
      - SANDBOX_ADMIN_TOKEN=${SANDBOX_ADMIN_TOKEN:-}

FILE: docs\xbrowser-architecture.md

# XBrowser execution architecture

Status: session, reference, shared execution, bounded macro, and host progress
layers implemented, with trusted task acceptance, sandbox egress/approval policy,
actual-token budgeting, and optional vision fallback. See
[the execution guide](xbrowser-execution.md), [security controls](xbrowser-security.md),
and the [validation report](xbrowser-validation.md) for measured deployment and model results.

## Ownership and boundaries

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    Executor[Local model executor] --> Registry[Tool registry and host permission policy]
    Registry --> Budget[Run action and wall-time budgets]
    Budget --> Client[Sandbox HTTP client]
    Client --> API[FastAPI validated contracts]
    API --> Controller[Shared action dispatcher]
    Controller --> Policy[Sandbox action capability policy]
    Controller --> Session[BrowserSession operation lock]
    Session --> Actions[Navigation, tabs, reference actions, keyboard, waits, scroll, evidence]
    Actions --> Chromium[Persistent Chromium context]
    API --> Macro[Bounded sequential macro runner]
    Macro --> Controller
    Actions --> Evidence[Structured action results and artifacts]
    Evidence --> Progress[Agent progress and compact working memory]
```

The model sees JSON tool arguments and compact text observations. It never owns
Playwright objects, profiles, locks, or arbitrary JavaScript. The sandbox is the
execution authority. Host permissions do not replace sandbox checks.

## Single execution path

The controller dispatches nine actions: open, observe, click, type, press, scroll,
tabs, wait, screenshot. Public single-action requests acquire the session lock
once. A macro holds that same lock across its entire sequence and invokes only
the controller's already-locked dispatcher. This avoids lock recursion and tab
changes from competing requests between macro steps.

Session owns lifecycle, profiles, tab IDs, and reference invalidation. It provides
internal navigation/tab primitives for the dispatcher, plus public lock-owning
helpers for non-HTTP callers. FastAPI handlers validate/translate requests and
return structured errors; they contain no separate implementation of an action.
Legacy endpoints remain aliases or adapters to the shared execution path.

Sandbox capabilities are server configuration, never model arguments. The
dispatcher checks the capability of every action, including every macro step.
Macros cannot invoke shell tools, lifecycle operations, evaluation, other macros,
or unknown actions. Navigation and artifact checks run through the existing
session methods. Domain/IP/redirect policy and sensitive-form authorization are
implemented as independent enforcement layers; see the security guide.

## Macro contract

`browser_run` accepts 1–20 typed sub-actions, a shared timeout (at most 90 seconds),
and an action allowance. The complete sequence is schema-validated before any
action starts. Runtime capability checks also apply per step. Execution is
sequential, stops on first failure, and records every attempted action. Results
include the failed index, attempted count, partial artifacts, and final state.
Successful earlier actions are never rolled back or replayed.

The run deadline includes waiting for the session lock and browser startup.
Each action also has a bounded timeout. Timeout/cancellation after dispatch has an
uncertain outcome and requires observation before deciding what to do next.
Cancellation propagates to the runner, releases the lock, and does not execute
later steps. Screenshot creation cleans partial files on cancellation.

The host caps macro length by remaining run actions and clamps its deadline by
remaining run time. Attempted sub-actions count toward the run budget. If the
transport loses the result, charge the full reserved macro allowance because the
number actually executed is unknown. A macro is one model call, not one action.

## State, history, and progress

Repeat signatures hash canonical complete arguments and browser scope/state;
they do not truncate arguments or exempt scrolling. Structured evidence of no
movement can additionally stop repeated scrolling even on a dynamically changing
page. Global action and time caps remain authoritative.

A tool success records action evidence and resets retry errors. It does not
advance a plan step. The executor explicitly emits `STEP_DONE:` after completing
the current objective; `DONE:` remains a model assertion about the whole task.
Neither assertion becomes deterministic task verification automatically.

Only the newest successful observation is retained in working memory. Superseded
observation payloads become short snapshot references; errors, non-observation
action outcomes, and artifact evidence remain. Full results stay in run records.

## Verification boundaries

Action verification answers mechanical questions: field value matches, tab
selected, scroll moved/reached an edge, file created, or wait condition satisfied.
Task verification must check acceptance criteria originating from the user's
request or trusted caller: required artifact count/contents, expected destination,
or report completeness. Implemented task-check contracts keep those criteria
separate from model-supplied assertions. Actions retain
`task_verification=not_evaluated`; trusted checks supply the completion decision.

## Implemented rollout gates

1. Complete shared action execution and bounded macros. Gate: local fixture
   navigate → five screenshots workflow, partial failures, concurrency, deadlines,
   cancellation, capability denials, and legacy compatibility.
2. Integrate host tools, action accounting, explicit plan progress, repeat guards,
   and observation compaction. Gate: scripted-model regression tests plus the
   existing suite; no LLM confidence needed to prove the mechanics.
3. Add trusted task acceptance checks and hardened navigation/sensitive-action
   policy. Gate: deterministic security and task-completion fixtures.
4. Validate Docker/Xvfb/noVNC, run repeated evaluations with the local model, and
   measure success, latency, tokens, and calls. Only then tune schema subsetting,
   tokenizer budgets, and optional visual marks.

Do not describe an implemented layer as production hardened or real-model proven
until its corresponding gate has been run.

FILE: docs\xbrowser-execution.md

# XBrowser actions and bounded workflows

The shared execution layer implements nine browser actions plus an optional macro
tool. The CLI and evaluation registries use this set by default. Legacy
`browser_navigate`/`browser_get_content` tool classes and HTTP endpoints remain
available to callers that still use them.

See [the architecture](xbrowser-architecture.md) for ownership, locks, verification
boundaries, and the completed validation gates.

## API and model surface

All routes use POST under `/tools/browser`. The model tool name is `browser_`
followed by the route name. Existing request arguments retain their meanings.

| Route | Main arguments | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `/open` | `url`, `new_tab`, `wait_for` | Final URL, HTTP status, host match |
| `/observe` | `scope`, `text`, `max_elements`, `max_chars` | Snapshot refs and page state |
| `/click` | `ref` + `snapshot_id`, or selector/text/role | Dispatched and before/after state |
| `/type` | Target, `text`, `clear_first`, `press_enter` | Value match; skip submission on mismatch |
| `/press` | `key` | Keyboard dispatch and resulting state |
| `/scroll` | `direction`, `pixels`, optional target, `wait_ms` | Position, moved, four edges |
| `/tabs` | `action`, `tab`, `url` | Active tab and registry |
| `/wait` | `condition`, `value`, `timeout_ms`, `milliseconds` | Condition satisfied or timeout |
| `/screenshot` | `path`, `selector`, `full_page` | Confined PNG path, existence and size |
| `/run` | Typed `steps`, `timeout_ms`, host `action_budget` | Per-action results and partial artifacts |

Scroll targets support a selector or snapshot reference for a nested scroll
container. Untargeted scrolling affects the document. Directions include up/down,
left/right, and top/bottom. Wait conditions are visible `text`, visible `selector`,
network `idle`, or a bounded `ms` delay. No arbitrary JavaScript wait predicate is
accepted. `idle` is optional because continuously active pages may never satisfy it.

`browser_tools(client, include_macro=False)` exposes exactly nine core tools.
The default enables the tenth `browser_run` tool. Old tool wrappers remain
importable but are not duplicated in the default model schema list.

## A deterministic capture workflow

The client can send a single macro:

```python
steps = [{"tool": "open", "args": {"url": url}}]
for index in range(5):
    if index:
        steps.append({"tool": "scroll", "args": {"pixels": 650}})
    steps.append({"tool": "screenshot", "args": {"path": f"evidence/page-{index}.png"}})
result = await client.browser_run(steps)
```

The server validates the whole request before execution and limits it to 20
actions. Each step uses the same action dispatcher as its single-action endpoint.
There are no nested macros, shell commands, lifecycle operations, or eval steps.
The lock covers the entire sequence. A failure stops subsequent steps, preserving
completed results and artifacts. `data.failed_index` is zero-based;
`data.attempted_actions` includes a failed dispatched step.

The shared deadline includes lock waiting and startup. The maximum is 90 seconds,
default 60; each action is also bounded to 30 seconds. Single HTTP actions have a
35-second boundary. The agent clamps the macro deadline and action allowance to
its remaining run budget and counts sub-actions rather than model calls. A lost
result conservatively consumes the entire reserved allowance.

Timeouts report uncertain outcomes: the last operation may already have produced
a side effect. Observe/reconcile before choosing the next action. Earlier steps
are not rolled back or replayed. Client disconnection cancels remaining macro
steps; an already dispatched browser side effect cannot be undone by cancellation.

## Sandbox policy

`XBROWSER_ALLOWED_ACTIONS` optionally lists comma-separated permitted core actions.
For example, `open,observe,tabs,wait,scroll,screenshot` denies click/type/press at
the sandbox dispatcher. An empty value denies all core actions. Lifecycle API
access still assumes the trusted local host. Macro capabilities are checked both
before the first side effect and at every step; legacy content access requires
the observe capability. HTTP bodies cannot grant capabilities.

The capability policy is paired with domain/IP, redirect/subresource and
sensitive-action controls. Private targets are denied by default. See
[the security guide](xbrowser-security.md) for exact local exceptions and approvals.

## Agent behavior and evidence

A successful action stays on the current plan objective. `STEP_DONE: <evidence>`
explicitly advances it after successful action evidence exists. `DONE:` remains a
model-declared final result; supplied trusted acceptance checks must pass before
the run can complete. Repeated completion claims without action evidence fail after bounded
nudges. A successful click or macro never independently verifies the user's goal.

Repeat signatures hash full canonical arguments and browser session/generation/
tab/state. Changed-state scrolling is allowed; repeated direct evidence of no
movement is independently bounded even if a dynamic page changes its fingerprint.
State capture includes bounded nested-container scroll positions.

Older successful observation payloads are compacted to snapshot references.
Errors, screenshots, and other action results stay in working memory subject to
the existing overall context limit. SQLite stores full tool output, structured
result metadata, and artifact paths. An additive `steps.result_metadata` migration
preserves existing run records. Action results report
`task_verification=not_evaluated`; the separate `/verify` endpoint and host task
verifier report whether the supplied criteria passed or failed.

The suite covers the five-screenshot workflow with real Chromium, partial failures,
schema/capability denials, lock waiting, cancellation/disconnection, new action
mechanics, host budget/progress behavior, and evidence persistence. Docker/Xvfb,
noVNC, actual tokenizer limits and local-model performance have separate recorded
results in the validation report.

FILE: docs\xbrowser-foundation.md

# XBrowser Foundation

This first increment implements session ownership, persistence, tabs, and screenshot
evidence. Chromium remains headful in Docker on `DISPLAY=:99`, visible through
noVNC. Tests use headless Chromium with local HTTP fixtures.

## How this fits XALGV

The host runs the planner/executor through `local_agent/core/loop.py`. Tools in
`local_agent/tools/` pass through the registry's permission policy, execute using
`local_agent/sandbox/client.py`, and return `ToolResult` plus action verification.
Working memory bounds conversation history; budgets bound the execution loop.
The core verifier falls back to an LLM when a tool has no verifier.

The sandbox runs a FastAPI tool server independently of the host. Its browser
router now delegates lifecycle and navigation to `BrowserSession`. Existing
content/click/type endpoints use the same context, active tab, and operation lock.
The browser owns all Playwright objects; neither the model nor the client sees them.
The Docker image copies the new `xbrowser` package beside the tool server.

The old screenshot request accidentally declared scroll fields and referenced
nonexistent `selector` and `full_page` attributes. It now has a validated screenshot
contract. Accessibility content uses Playwright locator ARIA snapshots rather than
the removed `page.accessibility` interface.

## Scope and lifecycle

One sandbox process owns one selected session scope. Configure these environment
variables before starting the server:

| Variable | Default | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `XBROWSER_WORKSPACE` | `/workspace` | Artifact and profile root |
| `XBROWSER_SCOPE` | `default` | Explicit persistent scope; letters, digits, `_`, `-` |
| `XBROWSER_HEADLESS` | `0` | Set to `1` for tests or a display-free server |

The profile is `<workspace>/.xbrowser/<scope>/profile`. Reusing a scope intentionally
retains cookies and local storage; use separate scopes for separate users or runs.
Only one process/worker may own a profile. This increment does not multiplex
independent scopes through a shared API server. The host must select a dedicated
sandbox/scope when it needs isolation across runs. Profiles are excluded from Git;
future document ingestion must explicitly exclude `.xbrowser` as well.

`session_id` identifies the controller lifetime; `generation` increments on every
Chromium launch. Tab IDs monotonically increase across launches within that
controller. A restarted server gets a new session ID. Consumers must use the tuple
`(session_id, generation, tab_id)` and discard earlier state after a restart.
Unexpected context closure or a renderer crash causes recovery before the next
operation, reported by `restarted=true`. Interrupted actions are never replayed.
Health does not launch Chromium. Server shutdown closes the context and driver.

All operations, including legacy endpoints, serialize on one lock. Popups become
active when registered. Closing the active tab selects the most recently registered
remaining tab. Closing the last tab leaves an empty registry until the next page
operation creates a new blank tab. No stale tab ID is repurposed.

## API

All browser routes use POST under `/tools/browser`:

| Route | Request |
| --- | --- |
| `/session` | `{"action":"start"}`; also `stop`, `restart`, `health` |
| `/open` | `{"url":"https://example.com","new_tab":false,"wait_for":"load"}` |
| `/tabs` | `{"action":"list"}`; `new`, `switch`, `close`, `back`, `forward`, `reload` |
| `/screenshot` | `{"path":"evidence/page.png","full_page":false,"selector":null}` |

`switch` and `close` require `tab`. `new` optionally accepts `url`. History actions
optionally accept a target `tab`, which also becomes active. `/navigate`,
`/get_content`, `/click`, and `/type` remain available to existing agent tools.
`/scroll` returns actual movement evidence. Request schema errors return HTTP 422;
browser failures return `success=false` with `error` and `error_code`.

Responses retain `success`, `output`, `error`, and `artifacts`. New fields include
session/generation identity, active tab ID, restart notification, and a structured
`data.tabs` registry. The host client preserves these fields in `ToolResult.metadata`.
New client methods are `browser_session`, `browser_open`, and `browser_tabs`.
Existing model tool names remain unchanged during this foundation increment.

`action_verification` carries mechanical evidence: final navigation URL and HTTP
status, selected tab, or screenshot existence and size. `task_verification` remains
`not_evaluated`: completing a browser command does not establish that the user's
research, report, or other objective has been completed. Objective checks belong
in the [trusted task verifier](xbrowser-security.md), which is now integrated.

## Current safeguards and boundaries

Explicit navigation accepts HTTP(S) without embedded credentials, plus
`about:blank`. Screenshots require PNG paths confined to the workspace, outside
browser profiles. Default names are unique; explicit existing files fail without
overwriting. The browser rejects downloads, dismisses dialogs (including prompts
and confirmations), and retains a bounded diagnostic event buffer. There is no
model-exposed JavaScript evaluation endpoint.

Domain/IP policies, pinned-IP proxying, redirect/subresource restrictions,
sensitive-form approvals, and ingestion exclusions are now implemented. Private
network access defaults to denied; tests opt into exact fixture origins. Browser
API access assumes the trusted local host, matching Compose's loopback-bound
ports. See [security and trusted completion](xbrowser-security.md) for boundaries.

Marks, snapshot-scoped references, and compact observations are now implemented;
see [the observation guide](xbrowser-observations.md). The
[execution layer](xbrowser-execution.md) adds bounded macros, state-aware repeat
detection, and explicit plan-step progress. Trusted task acceptance checks,
deployment, tokenizer, and local-model evaluation are covered by the validation report.

## Validation

Install the development and sandbox extras and Chromium:

```powershell
python -m venv .venv
.\.venv\Scripts\python -m pip install -e '.[dev,sandbox]'
$env:PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH = "$PWD\.venv\playwright-browsers"
.\.venv\Scripts\python -m playwright install chromium
.\.venv\Scripts\python -m pytest tests/xbrowser -q
.\.venv\Scripts\python -m pytest tests --ignore=tests/xbrowser -q
```

Browser tests require Chromium and fail if it cannot launch; they do not silently
skip integration coverage. Tests cover local navigation, stable tabs, popups,
persistent and isolated storage, restart recovery, locking/cancellation, dialog
dismissal, artifacts, and legacy compatibility. The end-to-end test calls the
actual sandbox FastAPI app through the agent's HTTP client using ASGI transport.
It uses real Chromium but does not exercise Docker networking or Xvfb/noVNC.

For container validation, start Docker Desktop, then rebuild with
`docker compose up -d --build sandbox`. Use one Uvicorn worker per selected profile.

FILE: docs\xbrowser-observations.md

# XBrowser observations and references

The second increment adds read-only element discovery, compact observations,
snapshot-scoped targeting, and deterministic click/type evidence. The existing
selector and text APIs remain available. `browser_observe` is registered in both
the normal CLI and evaluation tool registries.

## Using the API

POST `/tools/browser/observe` with an empty object for the default viewport view.
Options are `scope` (`viewport` or `page`), `text` (default false), `max_elements`
(1–100, default 30), and `max_chars` (800–8000, default 2400).

The response contains compact `output`, a top-level `snapshot_id` and
`state_after`, and structured `data.elements`, `data.scroll`, and counts/warnings.
The host preserves these fields in `ToolResult.metadata`. Frame IDs in each
element identify the originating frame; references automatically target it.

Use a returned reference and snapshot together:

```json
{"ref": 4, "snapshot_id": "s12-<controller-prefix>"}
```

Pass that object to `/tools/browser/click`, or add `text` to call
`/tools/browser/type`. Type retains `clear_first` and `press_enter` options. The
client methods accept the same keyword arguments. The host tool schemas now accept
either references or legacy selectors; type always requires `text`.

## Reference validity

References own exact Playwright element handles in server memory. Discovery does
not add attributes, globals, or event listeners to the page. Snapshot IDs include a
controller identifier to avoid reuse after server restart. Each snapshot is bound
to the session, browser generation, and tab. A new observation supersedes that
tab's previous snapshot; navigation of any of its frames invalidates it as well.

Before dispatch, the browser checks that the original element is still connected,
visible, enabled, uncovered, and has the expected identity (role, accessible-name
inputs, tag, type, ID, link/form target, and inline click handler). It also checks
that containing frames remain visible and uncovered. Playwright performs its own
actionability checks at execution time. A replacement element is never selected
using an old reference. There is no automatic retry of a dispatched action.

`STALE_REF` means observe again. `INVALID_REF` means the index was not reported in
the selected snapshot. `TARGET_NOT_ACTIONABLE` means the target or its frame is
disabled or occluded. Unrelated clocks/advertisements do not invalidate handles;
changing the target's identity does. Ordinary field-value updates are not identity
changes, allowing observe → type → click without an extra observation.

## Discovery and bounds

The implementation handles ordinary controls, common interactive ARIA roles,
open shadow roots, and accessible same-origin and cross-origin Playwright frames.
It filters hidden and covered controls, records disabled/checked state, and omits
password values. Native labels, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-label`, text, image alt
text, and common naming fallbacks supply labels. This is a practical subset of
accessible-name computation, not a complete implementation of the WAI algorithm.
Closed shadow roots are not traversed. Rotated/clipped/custom controls can require
the legacy locator fallback; coverage is sampled rather than exhaustively proven.

Discovery bounds traversal to 12,000 visited elements per frame, at most 16 frames,
and 120 retained candidates. It keeps at most eight tab snapshots. Output contains
only indices actually reported to the caller; undisplayed handles are disposed.
New observations and shutdown dispose previous handles. Limits appear as warnings
instead of pretending the page was fully indexed. Above/below counts describe
discovered frame-local candidates and are hints, not exhaustive page totals.

The default rendered output has a hard 2400-character limit. Text is opt-in and
shares that budget. Labels and URLs are quoted so website newlines do not create
fake observation fields. The response labels page data as untrusted, and the
executor prompt forbids treating page content as instructions or authorization.
`token_estimate` uses characters divided by four. With the checksum-pinned Qwen
tokenizer (included in Docker), `token_count` measures and enforces the 700-token
ceiling. Local runs without the asset explicitly retain the estimate label.

Click/type results include before/after state fingerprints. Fingerprints cover
bounded document text, non-password form state, scroll/URL/title, open shadow roots,
and up to 16 frames. They are evidence of change, not proof of task completion.
Clicks without immediate visible changes produce a warning. Type verifies the
resulting value and skips submission if the value does not match. An action that
completed while its page closed/navigated reports that subsequent state capture
was unavailable; the action is not replayed.

## Validation and remaining work

The Chromium fixtures cover labels, password redaction, covered/hidden/disabled
elements, DOM replacement, unrelated page updates, superseded snapshots, tab and
generation isolation, shadow DOM, same/cross-origin frames, and observation limits.
The API integration test runs observe → type → click through the host tool registry,
HTTP client, sandbox FastAPI app, and real Chromium. Run them with the setup in
[the foundation guide](xbrowser-foundation.md).

The [execution layer](xbrowser-execution.md) now provides the nine-action model API,
bounded macros, observation compaction, state-aware repeat detection, and explicit
plan-step progress. Trusted task acceptance, domain/IP and redirect policy,
sensitive-form approvals, marked screenshots and optional local vision analysis
are implemented. Docker/Xvfb and real-model measurements are recorded in the
validation report.


================================================================================
FILE: docs\xbrowser-security.md
================================================================================

# XBrowser security and trusted completion

XBrowser now enforces browser policy inside the sandbox, including individual
macro steps. These controls complement the host tool registry. They do not turn
arbitrary web applications into semantically read-only services.

## Network boundary

Chromium uses a browser-owned HTTP/CONNECT proxy. The proxy validates every
destination's domain, port, and DNS answers, then connects to a validated literal
IP rather than resolving the hostname again. TLS remains end to end. This closes
the check/connect DNS-rebinding gap and applies to redirects and subresources,
including redirects that Playwright does not independently route.

Private, loopback, metadata, reserved, multicast, and non-global addresses are
blocked by default. Encoded IPv4 forms, mapped IPv6, and standard IPv4 translation
forms are checked after resolution. Mixed public/private DNS answers fail closed.
Public destinations use ports 80/443. Browser service workers, WebSockets, WebRTC,
and QUIC are disabled to avoid alternate browser egress paths. The proxy bounds
active connections and request-header sizes. Tabs are limited to twelve.

Trusted server configuration:

| Setting | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `XBROWSER_ALLOWED_DOMAINS` | Comma-separated exact domains or explicit `*.domain` patterns; empty permits public domains |
| `XBROWSER_DENIED_DOMAINS` | Denials take precedence |
| `XBROWSER_LOCAL_ORIGINS` | Exact HTTP(S) origins for specifically trusted local services; default empty |
| `XBROWSER_ALLOWED_ACTIONS` | Optional core action capability list; an empty configured value denies all |
| `XBROWSER_ADMIN_TOKEN` | Enables authenticated, trusted approval issuance; unset disables it |

Local exceptions deliberately grant access to that origin and must be narrowly
configured. They cannot be supplied in browser tool arguments. The deployment
test temporarily uses only `http://127.0.0.1:8765` in a separate validation scope.
Production configuration is restored afterward.

## Sensitive interactions

Credential/payment controls and sensitive form interactions require a reviewed
approval. Non-GET/HEAD/OPTIONS browser requests are blocked unless an approved
action is currently executing. File uploads and downloads remain disabled.
Browser chrome, developer-tool, file-dialog, and system keyboard shortcuts are
blocked; document navigation/editing keys remain available. Password and common
payment values are omitted from structured observations.

Host applications can call `SandboxClient.browser_authorize(tool, args, admin_token)`
only after the person has reviewed that exact action. The `/authorize` endpoint
requires `X-XBrowser-Admin`. Its single-use token expires after 120 seconds and is
bound to the normalized arguments, session, generation, tab, and page fingerprint.
Use `client.use_browser_approval(token)` for the next click/type/press request.
An altered, replayed, expired, or stale grant fails closed. Model tool schemas
contain no approval-issuance method or administrative secret. Macros cannot carry
an approval blanket for a sequence.

Sensitive-control recognition is conservative and heuristic; HTTP method controls
provide an independent network boundary. Some poorly designed websites mutate
state through GET requests. Domain policy, target review, and acceptance criteria
are still needed; this implementation does not claim to infer every transaction's
business meaning. Native file choosers are intercepted without supplying files.

## Profiles, files, and media

Profiles remain in `.xbrowser/<scope>/profile`. They are excluded from Git and
document ingestion. Ordinary sandbox file APIs are confined to the workspace and
cannot read/write/list profiles or process-environment files outside that root.
Shell and Python execution now require the SYSTEM host tier and a sandbox-side
`X-Sandbox-Admin` credential. They default to disabled in the sandbox. Configure
matching `SANDBOX_ADMIN_TOKEN` (container) and `XALGV_SANDBOX_ADMIN_TOKEN` (host),
then explicitly opt in with `xalgv run ... --allow-execution` or supply a trusted
SYSTEM policy through the API. Python scripts use unique workspace staging paths.
Approved arbitrary execution is a separate trust boundary; browser-only controls
do not confine code that the person explicitly authorized at SYSTEM level.
Do not put administrative secrets in the model-readable workspace.

Artifact paths are resolved before use; symlinks cannot escape the workspace or
point into protected profiles. Screenshots use unique names or exclusive creation.
The image-transfer endpoint accepts only bounded workspace PNGs, limits dimensions,
and downsizes before sending pixels to the configured local vision model. Marked
screenshots are drawn onto bitmap copies; the page DOM is not annotated.

All website text, diagnostics, and vision output remain untrusted page data.
Neither content nor model confidence can mint approval or satisfy a task check.

## Trusted task acceptance

Supply criteria through the trusted caller:

```python
loop = AgentLoop(config, models, registry, memory,
                 task_verifier=TaskVerifier(client))
record = await loop.run(goal, acceptance_checks=[
    {"kind": "url", "expected": "https://example.com/"},
    {"kind": "artifact", "path": "evidence/page.png", "png": True,
     "width": 1280, "height": 720},
    {"kind": "artifact", "path": "report.md", "contains": "Required findings"},
])
```

Alternatively, save the checks as a JSON list (or `{"checks": [...]}`) and run
`xalgv run "your goal" --acceptance checks.json`. Supported checks cover exact
active URL, visible text, tab count, file existence/minimum size, UTF-8 contents,
SHA-256, PNG decoding, and PNG dimensions. All checks must pass.

The loop copies the trusted criteria before execution. A model's `DONE:` triggers
the checker rather than overriding it. Failed completion claims allow bounded
repair attempts and eventually fail. SQLite records the criteria, checker evidence,
action metadata, and full outputs. Runs without supplied criteria are explicitly
labelled `not_evaluated`/`model_assertion`; they are not called deterministically
verified. Criteria check the properties explicitly supplied, not report quality
that nobody specified.

## Token budget and visual fallback

The Docker image installs the official Qwen 3.5 tokenizer with its SHA-256 pinned
in `sandbox/xbrowser/tokenizer_config.json`. Observations keep only reported refs,
remove optional text/extra elements as necessary, and stay below 700 actual tokens.
For local development, install the sandbox extras and run:

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python -m sandbox.xbrowser.token_budget --download
```

Without a tokenizer asset, local observations explicitly report a character
estimate instead of claiming a measured token cap. `XBROWSER_TOKENIZER_PATH` can
point to the checksum-matching asset. The shipped tokenizer targets Qwen 3.5;
changing model families requires a corresponding tokenizer configuration update.

`browser_screenshot(marks=true)` returns a numbered PNG and snapshot refs.
`browser_screenshot(describe=true)` additionally asks the configured local vision
role to interpret a confined screenshot. It preserves artifacts on inference
failure. Vision text does not grant authorization or replace deterministic checks.
The default vision role uses the same Qwen 3.5 9B model as the executor.
Planner, executor, and vision defaults share the measured 8192-token context and
temperature zero profile, avoiding model reloads just to change roles.


================================================================================
FILE: docs\xbrowser-validation.md
================================================================================

# XBrowser v1 validation report

Validated on 8 November 2026. The remaining v1 implementation and defined validation
gates are complete. Browser sessions, references, nine actions, macros, trusted task
checks, sandbox network/approval controls, real-token budgets, marked screenshots,
and optional local vision analysis are implemented.

Follow-up from a real container restart: stale X display files and Chromium
singleton links exposed a restart case missed by the initial smoke tests. Startup
now removes stale display files, waits for readiness, supervises all services,
checks the live VNC backend, and closes Chromium before stopping the display.
Profile recovery holds an exclusive scope lock and refuses to disturb live
Chromium owners. Planner/executor tool lists are now permission-filtered, and an
unadvertised shell choice is returned for model correction rather than executed.
Focused regressions and a managed capture of the reported site passed.

| Gate | Recorded result |
| --- | --- |
| Full regression suite | 230 passed in 116.10 seconds |
| Ruff checks on XBrowser and host integration | Passed; whitespace/diff checks passed |
| Original real-model fixture suite | 9/9 passed: navigation, reference form, five captures; three repetitions each |
| Optimized capture workflow | 3/3 passed; exactly three model calls per trial |
| Capture correctness | One macro, correct ten-action order, five valid 1280×720 viewport PNGs |
| Optimized capture latency | Median 76.1s; nearest-rank p95 99.9s |
| Docker deployment | All 15 checks passed; deployed source hashes match local source |
| Display and viewing | Headful Chromium, 1280×720 Xvfb, noVNC HTML and live RFB WebSocket bridge passed |
| Real vision fallback | Qwen interpreted a marked PNG from the Docker browser |
| Observation budgeting | Official checksum-pinned Qwen tokenizer; 700-token limit enforced, including multilingual fixtures |
| Default-policy restoration | Temporary local fixture exception removed; environment reads and unauthenticated execution blocked |

The model was the locally installed Qwen 3.5 9B Q4_K_M model at 8192 context and
temperature zero. Hardware was an RTX 3050 Laptop GPU with 6 GB VRAM, with partial
CPU offload. The default planner/executor/vision profile now matches those settings.
Timings are specific to this machine and these tasks.

## Evidence

- [Aggregate machine-readable results](../traces/xbrowser-validation/validation-summary.json)
- [Full regression output](../traces/xbrowser-validation/pytest-final.txt)
- [Real-model task measurements](../traces/xbrowser-validation/model-report.json)
- [Optimized capture measurements](../traces/xbrowser-validation/capture-optimized-report.json)
- [Docker/display/source checks](../traces/xbrowser-validation/deployment-report.json)
- [Real vision result and token usage](../traces/xbrowser-validation/vision-deployment-report.json)
- [Restored normal configuration](../traces/xbrowser-validation/production-report.json)

The initial model trial exposed completion markers following explanation; parsing
was corrected and its earlier failed report retained. A separate optimization run
recorded an Ollama HTTP 500 while validation workloads overlapped; its report was
retained, and the final isolated three-trial capture run passed. The one-off public
HTTPS transport check also demonstrated that an obsolete live-page body assertion
correctly fails even when navigation succeeds. Public sites are not used for the
repeatable model fixture suite.

These are measured results on three controlled scenarios, not evidence of a 100%
open-web success rate. The sensitive-action heuristic cannot infer every site's
business semantics; GET side effects and approved SYSTEM code remain explicit
trust boundaries. See [security and verification](xbrowser-security.md).

## Deployment and use

The sandbox is running at [the local API](http://localhost:9000/docs) and
[noVNC](http://localhost:6080/vnc.html), using the default scope and strict default
network policy. Temporary test-service exceptions are not left enabled. Docker's
startup was repaired by backing up its two stale temporary sockets through WSL;
no Docker factory reset was used.

Ordinary browser work needs no administration secret. Sensitive browser actions
use reviewed, single-use approvals. Shell/Python tools now require SYSTEM authority
and sandbox administration credentials; this intentionally closes their former
workspace-write bypass. Use `--allow-execution` only for a run explicitly trusted
to execute arbitrary code. Administrative secrets should stay outside the workspace.

For deterministic completion, supply trusted checks with `--acceptance checks.json`.
Without criteria, the application explicitly records model-asserted completion as
`not_evaluated`. The exact acceptance checks determine what has actually been proven.

## Reproduction

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python -m pip install -e '.[dev,sandbox]'
$env:PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH = "$PWD\.venv\playwright-browsers"
.\.venv\Scripts\python -m playwright install chromium
.\.venv\Scripts\python -m sandbox.xbrowser.token_budget --download
.\.venv\Scripts\python -m pytest tests -q
.\.venv\Scripts\python -m eval.xbrowser --model qwen3.5:9b --repeats 3
.\.venv\Scripts\python -m eval.xbrowser --scenarios five_captures --repeats 3 --output traces/captures.json
docker compose up -d --build sandbox
```

The deployment smoke script requires the exact fixture origin described in its
module documentation. Run it in a separate validation scope, then restore the
normal Compose environment. `--vision-model qwen3.5:9b` adds the real vision check.

"""Evaluation subsystem: harness, checkers, and task definitions."""


================================================================================
FILE: eval\deployment_smoke.py
================================================================================

"""Smoke checks against an already running Docker sandbox with local fixtures.

The validation deployment must explicitly allow http://127.0.0.1:8765 and serve
eval/fixtures/profile.html there. No domains/private networks are globally relaxed.
"""

import argparse
import asyncio
import hashlib
import json
import subprocess
import uuid
from pathlib import Path

import httpx
import websockets

from local_agent.config import AgentConfig, ModelRoleConfig
from local_agent.models import ModelManager
from local_agent.sandbox.client import SandboxClient
from local_agent.tools.browser import BrowserScreenshotTool


async def smoke(args):
    checks = {}
    client = SandboxClient(args.base_url)
    try:
        root = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
        source_files = [root / "sandbox" / name for name in ("tool_server.py", "browser_server.py")]
        source_files += [path for path in (root / "sandbox" / "xbrowser").iterdir() if path.suffix in {".py", ".json"}]
        expected = {str(path.relative_to(root / "sandbox")).replace("\\", "/"): hashlib.sha256(path.read_bytes()).hexdigest() for path in source_files}
        code = "import pathlib,hashlib,json; root=pathlib.Path('/opt/tool_server'); files=[root/'tool_server.py',root/'browser_server.py']+[p for p in (root/'xbrowser').iterdir() if p.suffix in {'.py','.json'}]; print(json.dumps({str(p.relative_to(root)):hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in files}))"
        manifest = await asyncio.to_thread(subprocess.run, ["docker", "exec", "xalgv_sandbox", "python3", "-c", code],
                                           capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10, check=True)
        checks["deployed_source_matches"] = json.loads(manifest.stdout) == expected
        for _ in range(60):
            if await client.health_check():
                break
            await asyncio.sleep(0.5)
        checks["api_health"] = await client.health_check()
        async with httpx.AsyncClient() as http:
            response = await http.get(args.novnc_url + "/vnc.html")
            checks["novnc_html"] = response.status_code == 200
        async with websockets.connect(args.novnc_url.replace("http://", "ws://") + "/websockify", subprotocols=["binary"]) as socket:
            greeting = await asyncio.wait_for(socket.recv(), 5)
            checks["novnc_rfb_bridge"] = greeting.startswith(b"RFB ") if isinstance(greeting, bytes) else greeting.startswith("RFB ")
        display = await asyncio.to_thread(subprocess.run, ["docker", "exec", "xalgv_sandbox", "xwininfo", "-root"],
                                          capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10, check=False)
        checks["xvfb_display"] = display.returncode == 0 and "1280" in display.stdout and "720" in display.stdout
        first = await client.browser_open(args.fixture_url)
        checks["headful_navigation"] = first.success
        first_id = first.metadata.get("tab_id")
        second = await client.browser_tabs("new", url=args.fixture_url)
        checks["tabs"] = second.success and second.metadata.get("tab_id") != first_id
        switched = await client.browser_tabs("switch", tab=first_id)
        checks["switch"] = switched.success and switched.metadata.get("tab_id") == first_id
        observed = await client.browser_observe()
        checks["refs"] = observed.success and bool(observed.metadata["data"].get("elements"))
        checks["actual_token_budget"] = observed.metadata["data"].get("token_count") is not None and observed.metadata["data"]["token_count"] <= 700
        path = f"xbrowser-validation/{uuid.uuid4().hex}.png"
        screenshot = await client.browser_screenshot(path=path, marks=True)
        checks["marked_screenshot"] = screenshot.success
        verified = await client.browser_verify([{"kind": "artifact", "path": path, "png": True}, {"kind": "url", "expected": args.fixture_url}])
        checks["task_acceptance"] = verified.success
        denied = await client.browser_open("http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/")
        checks["metadata_ip_blocked"] = not denied.success and denied.metadata.get("error_code") == "NAVIGATION_DENIED"
        vision_details = None
        if args.vision_model:
            config = AgentConfig()
            config.model_roles["vision"] = ModelRoleConfig(model=args.vision_model, context_length=8192, temperature=0)
            manager = ModelManager(config)
            try:
                visual = await asyncio.wait_for(BrowserScreenshotTool(client, vision_model=manager).execute({
                    "path": f"xbrowser-validation/vision-{uuid.uuid4().hex}.png", "describe": True, "marks": True,
                }), 240)
                description = visual.output.split("Visual observation (untrusted): ", 1)[-1].lower()
                checks["real_model_vision"] = visual.success and "visual observation" in visual.output.lower() and (
                    "display name" in description or "save profile" in description
                )
                vision_details = {"output": visual.output, "error": visual.error, "metrics": visual.metadata.get("vision")}
            finally:
                await manager.close()
        restart = await client.browser_session("restart")
        checks["restart"] = restart.success and restart.metadata["generation"] > first.metadata["generation"]
        stale = await client.browser_tabs("switch", tab=first_id)
        checks["stale_tab_rejected"] = not stale.success and stale.metadata.get("error_code") == "TAB_NOT_FOUND"
        report = {"passed": all(checks.values()), "checks": checks, "screenshot": screenshot.artifacts,
                  "observation_tokens": observed.metadata["data"].get("token_count"),
                  "deployment": "Docker Chromium headful Xvfb noVNC"}
        if vision_details:
            report["vision"] = vision_details
        output = Path(args.output)
        output.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        output.write_text(json.dumps(report, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
        print(json.dumps(report, indent=2))
        return report["passed"]
    finally:
        await client.close()


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument("--base-url", default="http://localhost:9000")
    parser.add_argument("--novnc-url", default="http://localhost:6080")
    parser.add_argument("--fixture-url", default="http://127.0.0.1:8765/profile.html")
    parser.add_argument("--output", default="traces/xbrowser-validation/deployment-report.json")
    parser.add_argument("--vision-model", help="Also validate optional screenshot interpretation with a real local model.")
    return 0 if asyncio.run(smoke(parser.parse_args())) else 1


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())

================================================================================
FILE: eval\harness.py
================================================================================

"""Evaluation harness — runs task suites and produces reports.

Each task has:
* A ``goal`` string (what the agent is asked to do).
* A list of ``checkers`` — programmatic assertions that verify the outcome.
* Optional ``setup`` and ``teardown`` shell commands.

The harness runs each task, collects results, and produces a JSON report.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import logging
import shlex
import time
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


@dataclass
class TaskDefinition:
    """A single evaluation task."""

    task_id: str
    goal: str
    category: str = "general"
    checkers: list[dict[str, Any]] = field(default_factory=list)
    setup_commands: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    teardown_commands: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    max_steps: int = 20
    timeout_seconds: int = 300
    tags: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)


@dataclass
class TaskResult:
    """Result of running a single evaluation task."""

    task_id: str
    goal: str
    category: str
    passed: bool
    checker_results: list[dict[str, Any]] = field(default_factory=list)
    steps_taken: int = 0
    wall_time_seconds: float = 0.0
    error: str | None = None
    agent_summary: str = ""


@dataclass
class EvalReport:
    """Summary report for an evaluation run."""

    total_tasks: int = 0
    passed: int = 0
    failed: int = 0
    errors: int = 0
    results: list[TaskResult] = field(default_factory=list)
    wall_time_seconds: float = 0.0

    @property
    def success_rate(self) -> float:
        return self.passed / self.total_tasks if self.total_tasks > 0 else 0.0

    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return {
            "total_tasks": self.total_tasks,
            "passed": self.passed,
            "failed": self.failed,
            "errors": self.errors,
            "success_rate": round(self.success_rate, 3),
            "wall_time_seconds": round(self.wall_time_seconds, 1),
            "results": [asdict(r) for r in self.results],
        }


class EvalHarness:
    """Runs evaluation tasks against the agent and collects results."""

    def __init__(self, tasks_dir: Path | str | None = None) -> None:
        self._tasks_dir = Path(tasks_dir) if tasks_dir else Path(__file__).parent / "tasks"

    def load_tasks(self, category: str | None = None) -> list[TaskDefinition]:
        """Load task definitions from JSON files in the tasks directory."""
        tasks: list[TaskDefinition] = []
        if not self._tasks_dir.exists():
            logger.warning("Tasks directory not found: %s", self._tasks_dir)
            return tasks

        for path in sorted(self._tasks_dir.glob("*.json")):
            try:
                data = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
                file_tasks = data if isinstance(data, list) else data.get("tasks", [data])
                for item in file_tasks:
                    if not item.get("checkers") and item.get("verification"):
                        item["checkers"] = [item["verification"]]
                    task = TaskDefinition(**{k: v for k, v in item.items() if k in TaskDefinition.__dataclass_fields__})
                    if category is None or task.category == category:
                        tasks.append(task)
            except Exception as exc:
                logger.warning("Failed to load %s: %s", path, exc)

        return tasks

    async def run_task(self, task: TaskDefinition, agent_loop: Any) -> TaskResult:
        """Run a single task and return the result."""
        start = time.monotonic()
        try:
            run_record = await agent_loop.run(task.goal)
            elapsed = time.monotonic() - start

            # Run checkers.
            checker_results = []
            all_passed = run_record.status.value == "completed" and bool(task.checkers)
            for checker_def in task.checkers:
                checker_result = await self._run_checker(checker_def, agent_loop, run_record)
                checker_results.append(checker_result)
                if not checker_result.get("passed", False):
                    all_passed = False

            if not task.checkers:
                checker_results.append({"passed": False, "error": "No objective checkers configured; model completion alone is insufficient"})

            return TaskResult(
                task_id=task.task_id,
                goal=task.goal,
                category=task.category,
                passed=all_passed,
                checker_results=checker_results,
                steps_taken=len(run_record.steps),
                wall_time_seconds=elapsed,
                agent_summary=run_record.summary,
            )
        except Exception as exc:
            elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
            return TaskResult(
                task_id=task.task_id,
                goal=task.goal,
                category=task.category,
                passed=False,
                wall_time_seconds=elapsed,
                error=f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}",
            )

    async def run_suite(self, agent_loop: Any, category: str | None = None) -> EvalReport:
        """Run all tasks and produce a report."""
        tasks = self.load_tasks(category)
        report = EvalReport(total_tasks=len(tasks))
        suite_start = time.monotonic()

        for task in tasks:
            logger.info("Running task: %s — %s", task.task_id, task.goal)
            result = await self.run_task(task, agent_loop)
            report.results.append(result)
            if result.error:
                report.errors += 1
            elif result.passed:
                report.passed += 1
            else:
                report.failed += 1

        report.wall_time_seconds = time.monotonic() - suite_start
        return report

    async def _run_checker(self, checker_def: dict[str, Any], agent_loop: Any, run_record=None) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Run a single checker.  Checkers are shell commands that exit 0 on pass."""
        checker_type = checker_def.get("type", "shell")
        if checker_type == "output_contains":
            text = "\n".join(step.result_output for step in run_record.steps) if run_record else ""
            expected = checker_def.get("expected", [])
            return {"type": checker_type, "passed": bool(expected) and all(value in text for value in expected)}
        if checker_type == "shell":
            command = checker_def.get("command", "")
            description = checker_def.get("description", command[:80])
            # Execute the check command via the sandbox.
            try:
                from local_agent.tools.base import ToolResult
                result = await agent_loop._tools.execute("shell_exec", {"command": command})
                passed = result.success and (result.exit_code == 0)
                return {"type": "shell", "description": description, "passed": passed, "output": result.output[:500]}
            except Exception as exc:
                return {"type": "shell", "description": description, "passed": False, "error": str(exc)}
        elif checker_type == "file_exists":
            path = checker_def.get("path", "")
            try:
                result = await agent_loop._tools.execute("shell_exec", {"command": f"test -f {shlex.quote(path)} && echo EXISTS"})
                return {"type": "file_exists", "path": path, "passed": result.success}
            except Exception as exc:
                return {"type": "file_exists", "path": path, "passed": False, "error": str(exc)}

        return {"type": checker_type, "passed": False, "error": "Unknown checker type."}

    def save_report(self, report: EvalReport, output_path: Path | str) -> Path:
        """Save the report as JSON."""
        path = Path(output_path)
        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        path.write_text(json.dumps(report.to_dict(), indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
        logger.info("Report saved to %s", path)
        return path

================================================================================

"""Repeated local-model evaluation against real Chromium and objective criteria.

Run: python -m eval.xbrowser --model qwen3.5:9b --repeats 3
No external websites, fabricated model responses, or summary-only success checks.
"""

import argparse
import asyncio
import json
import math
import statistics
import threading
import time
import uuid
from functools import partial
from http.server import SimpleHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
from pathlib import Path

import httpx

from local_agent.config import AgentConfig, ModelRoleConfig
from local_agent.core.loop import AgentLoop
from local_agent.core.task_verification import TaskVerifier
from local_agent.memory.store import MemoryStore
from local_agent.models import ModelManager
from local_agent.sandbox.client import SandboxClient
from local_agent.security.permissions import PermissionPolicy, PermissionTier
from local_agent.tools.browser import browser_tools
from local_agent.tools.registry import ToolRegistry
from sandbox import browser_server, tool_server
from sandbox.xbrowser import BrowserSession
from sandbox.xbrowser.safety import BrowserPolicy


class Meter:
    def __init__(self, manager):
        self.manager = manager
        self.calls = []

    async def chat(self, *args, **kwargs):
        started = time.monotonic()
        try:
            response = await self.manager.chat(*args, **kwargs)
        except Exception as exc:
            self.calls.append({"role": args[0], "seconds": time.monotonic() - started,
                               "prompt_tokens": 0, "generated_tokens": 0, "tools": [], "failed": True,
                               "error": str(exc)})
            raise
        self.calls.append({"role": args[0], "seconds": time.monotonic() - started,
                           "prompt_tokens": response.prompt_eval_count, "generated_tokens": response.eval_count,
                           "tools": [call.name for call in response.tool_calls]})
        return response


def percentile(values, fraction):
    return sorted(values)[min(len(values)-1, max(0, math.ceil(len(values)*fraction)-1))] if values else None


async def evaluate(args):
    output = Path(args.output).resolve()
    output.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    workspace = output.parent / "workspace"
    workspace.mkdir(exist_ok=True)

    class Handler(SimpleHTTPRequestHandler):
        def log_message(self, *_):
            pass

    server = ThreadingHTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", 0), partial(Handler, directory=str(Path(__file__).parent / "fixtures")))
    thread = threading.Thread(target=server.serve_forever, daemon=True)
    thread.start()
    url = f"http://127.0.0.1:{server.server_port}/profile.html"
    config = AgentConfig(db_path=output.parent / "evaluation.db", trace_dir=output.parent / "checkpoints",
                         max_steps=20, max_wall_time_seconds=args.timeout, vram_budget_mb=6144)
    for role in ("planner", "executor", "vision"):
        config.model_roles[role] = ModelRoleConfig(model=args.model, context_length=8192, temperature=0)
    manager = ModelManager(config)
    report = {"model": args.model, "repeats": args.repeats, "mode": "real-model-real-chromium-local-fixtures",
              "context_length": 8192, "results": [], "status": "running"}
    output.write_text(json.dumps(report, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
    try:
        for repeat in range(args.repeats):
            for scenario in args.scenarios:
                trial = f"{scenario}-{repeat}-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}"
                session = BrowserSession(workspace, scope=trial, headless=True)
                session.policy = BrowserPolicy(local_origins=(f"http://127.0.0.1:{server.server_port}",))
                browser_server.session = session
                client = SandboxClient("http://sandbox")
                await client.close()
                client._client = httpx.AsyncClient(transport=httpx.ASGITransport(app=tool_server.app), base_url="http://sandbox")
                registry = ToolRegistry(PermissionPolicy(auto_approve_up_to=PermissionTier.NETWORK))
                registry.register_many(*browser_tools(client))
                meter = Meter(manager)
                loop = AgentLoop(config, meter, registry, MemoryStore(config.db_path), task_verifier=TaskVerifier(client))
                checks = [{"kind": "url", "expected": url}]
                if scenario == "navigation":
                    goal = f"Open {url}, observe the page, and report its title. Then finish."
                    checks.append({"kind": "text", "expected": "Evaluation profile"})
                elif scenario == "reference_form":
                    goal = f"Open {url}, use browser_observe references to type Ada Lovelace into Display name, click Save profile, and verify Saved Ada Lovelace appears."
                    checks.append({"kind": "text", "expected": "Saved Ada Lovelace"})
                else:
                    paths = [f"{trial}/page-{index}.png" for index in range(5)]
                    goal = f"Use exactly ONE browser_run to open {url}, immediately take the first viewport PNG screenshot, then alternate scroll down 500 pixels and viewport screenshot four times. Paths in order: {json.dumps(paths)}. Use full_page=false. The macro contains exactly 10 steps: open, screenshot, scroll, screenshot, scroll, screenshot, scroll, screenshot, scroll, screenshot. Do not navigate or observe separately. When all five files exist, respond DONE."
                    checks.extend({"kind": "artifact", "path": path, "png": True, "width": 1280, "height": 720} for path in paths)
                started = time.monotonic()
                try:
                    record = await loop.run(goal, acceptance_checks=checks)
                    # Independently recheck after the loop, including when it stopped early.
                    verified = await client.browser_verify(checks)
                    passed = record.status.value == "completed" and verified.success
                    tools = [step.tool_name for step in record.steps if step.action_type == "tool_call"]
                    if scenario == "reference_form":
                        passed = passed and all(any(step.tool_name == tool and "ref" in (step.tool_params or {}) for step in record.steps)
                                                for tool in ("browser_click", "browser_type"))
                    workflow_correct = True
                    efficient = True
                    if scenario == "five_captures":
                        macros = [step for step in record.steps if step.tool_name == "browser_run"]
                        sequence = macros[0].tool_params["steps"] if len(macros) == 1 else []
                        workflow_correct = [step["tool"] for step in sequence] == ["open", "screenshot", "scroll", "screenshot", "scroll", "screenshot", "scroll", "screenshot", "scroll", "screenshot"]
                        efficient = len(meter.calls) <= 3
                        passed = passed and workflow_correct
                    report["results"].append({"trial": trial, "scenario": scenario, "passed": passed,
                        "run_status": record.status.value, "summary": record.summary, "seconds": time.monotonic()-started,
                        "model_calls": len(meter.calls), "prompt_tokens": sum(call["prompt_tokens"] for call in meter.calls),
                        "generated_tokens": sum(call["generated_tokens"] for call in meter.calls), "tools": tools,
                        "calls": meter.calls, "acceptance": verified.metadata,
                        "workflow_correct": workflow_correct, "three_call_target_met": efficient})
                    print(f"{trial}: {'PASS' if passed else 'FAIL'} ({len(meter.calls)} model calls)", flush=True)
                    output.write_text(json.dumps(report, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
                finally:
                    await session.control("stop")
                    await client.close()
        report["status"] = "complete"
        results = report["results"]
        report["success_rate"] = sum(item["passed"] for item in results) / len(results)
        report["target_success_rate"] = 0.9
        report["target_met"] = report["success_rate"] >= 0.9
        captures = [item for item in results if item["scenario"] == "five_captures"]
        report["capture_three_call_target_met"] = all(item["three_call_target_met"] for item in captures)
        report["median_seconds"] = statistics.median(item["seconds"] for item in results)
        report["p95_seconds"] = percentile([item["seconds"] for item in results], 0.95)
        report["median_model_calls"] = statistics.median(item["model_calls"] for item in results)
        report["running_models"] = await manager.list_running()
        output.write_text(json.dumps(report, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
    finally:
        await manager.close()
        server.shutdown()
        server.server_close()
        thread.join()
    return report


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument("--model", default="qwen3.5:9b")
    parser.add_argument("--repeats", type=int, default=3)
    parser.add_argument("--timeout", type=int, default=240)
    parser.add_argument("--scenarios", nargs="+", choices=["navigation", "reference_form", "five_captures"], default=["navigation", "reference_form", "five_captures"])
    parser.add_argument("--output", default="traces/xbrowser-validation/model-report.json")
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.repeats < 1:
        parser.error("repeats must be positive")
    report = asyncio.run(evaluate(args))
    print(json.dumps({key: report[key] for key in ("success_rate", "target_met", "median_seconds", "median_model_calls")}))
    return 0 if report["target_met"] else 1


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())


================================================================================
FILE: local_agent\__init__.py
================================================================================

"""XALGV — Local Autonomous Computer-Using Agent.

A local-first, sandboxed, multimodal computer-using agent with dynamic
planning, tool execution, browser automation, document intelligence,
vision, translation, and custom-model integration.
"""

__version__ = "0.1.0"


================================================================================
FILE: local_agent\config.py
================================================================================

"""Centralised configuration for the local agent."""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
from dataclasses import dataclass, field, replace
from pathlib import Path


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ModelRoleConfig:
    """Maps a logical role to a concrete Ollama model tag."""

    model: str
    context_length: int = 8192
    temperature: float = 0.6
    thinking: bool = False  # Use /think mode for this role


# Tool-use roles share one model/context to avoid role-switch reloads.
# DEFAULT_MODEL_ROLES: dict[str, ModelRoleConfig] = {
#     "planner": ModelRoleConfig(model="qwen3:8b", context_length=8192, temperature=0.6, thinking=True),
#     "executor": ModelRoleConfig(model="qwen3:8b", context_length=8192, temperature=0.3),
#     "router": ModelRoleConfig(model="qwen3:1.7b", context_length=4096, temperature=0.1),
#     "vision": ModelRoleConfig(model="qwen2.5-vl:7b", context_length=4096, temperature=0.3),
#     "embed": ModelRoleConfig(model="bge-m3", context_length=8192, temperature=0.0),
#     "translate": ModelRoleConfig(model="qwen3:8b", context_length=8192, temperature=0.3),
# }
DEFAULT_MODEL_ROLES: dict[str, ModelRoleConfig] = {
    "planner": ModelRoleConfig(
        model="gemma4:e4b",
        context_length=8192,
        temperature=0.0,
        thinking=False
    ),

    "executor": ModelRoleConfig(
        model="gemma4:e4b",
        # context_length=4096,
        # temperature=0.3
        context_length=8192,
        temperature=0.0,
        thinking=False
    ),

    "router": ModelRoleConfig(
        model="gemma4:e4b",
        context_length=4096,
        temperature=0.1
    ),

    "vision": ModelRoleConfig(
        model="gemma4:e4b",
        context_length=8192,
        temperature=0.0
    ),

    "embed": ModelRoleConfig(
        model="gemma4:e4b",
        context_length=8192,
        temperature=0.0
    ),

    "translate": ModelRoleConfig(
        model="gemma4:e4b",
        context_length=8192,
        temperature=0.3
    ),
}

@dataclass
class AgentConfig:
    """Top-level agent configuration.  All paths default to sensible locations
    relative to the project root, and every value can be overridden through
    environment variables prefixed with ``XALGV_``."""

    # ── Ollama ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    ollama_base_url: str = "http://localhost:11434"
    vram_budget_mb: int = 8192
    model_roles: dict[str, ModelRoleConfig] = field(default_factory=lambda: dict(DEFAULT_MODEL_ROLES))

    # ── Sandbox ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    sandbox_url: str = "http://localhost:9000"
    sandbox_container: str = "xalgv_sandbox"

    # ── Budgets & limits ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
    max_steps: int = 50
    max_wall_time_seconds: int = 600
    max_retries_per_step: int = 3
    max_repeated_actions: int = 3

    # ── Paths ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    project_root: Path = field(default_factory=lambda: Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent)
    workspace_dir: Path = field(default=None)  # type: ignore[assignment]
    data_dir: Path = field(default=None)  # type: ignore[assignment]
    db_path: Path = field(default=None)  # type: ignore[assignment]
    trace_dir: Path = field(default=None)  # type: ignore[assignment]

    # ── Search ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    searxng_base_url: str = "http://localhost:8080"

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.workspace_dir is None:
            self.workspace_dir = self.project_root / "workspace"
        if self.data_dir is None:
            self.data_dir = self.project_root / "data"
        if self.db_path is None:
            self.db_path = self.project_root / "data" / "agent.db"
        if self.trace_dir is None:
            self.trace_dir = self.project_root / "traces"

    # ── Helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    def ensure_dirs(self) -> None:
        """Create all required directories if they do not exist."""
        self.workspace_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        self.data_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        self.db_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        self.trace_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    def use_generation_model(self, model_tag: str) -> None:
        """Override text/image generation roles without changing the embedder."""
        for role, role_config in self.model_roles.items():
            if role != "embed":
                self.model_roles[role] = replace(role_config, model=model_tag)

    @classmethod
    def from_env(cls) -> AgentConfig:
        """Build a config using ``XALGV_*`` environment variables as overrides."""
        overrides: dict[str, object] = {}
        env_map = {
            "XALGV_OLLAMA_URL": "ollama_base_url",
            "XALGV_SANDBOX_URL": "sandbox_url",
            "XALGV_VRAM_MB": ("vram_budget_mb", int),
            "XALGV_MAX_STEPS": ("max_steps", int),
            "XALGV_MAX_TIME": ("max_wall_time_seconds", int),
            "XALGV_SEARXNG_URL": "searxng_base_url",
            "XALGV_DB_PATH": ("db_path", Path),
            "XALGV_WORKSPACE": ("workspace_dir", Path),
            "XALGV_TRACE_DIR": ("trace_dir", Path),
        }
        for env_key, spec in env_map.items():
            value = os.environ.get(env_key)
            if value is None:
                continue
            if isinstance(spec, str):
                overrides[spec] = value
            else:
                attr_name, converter = spec
                overrides[attr_name] = converter(value)
        return cls(**overrides)
